# Task
You will be given a **Requirement** and a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML. Your task is to annotate every operation listed in <Operations> with a specfication.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pesudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
Either in a school that performs the following functions: Develop catalogs that describe the courses and give the estimated dates of the sessions (date format : "2025-02-06"). Each course has an unique id. Register people have their unique id and can participate in the sessions. Determine the trainers who will lead the sessions. Each trainer has an unique id. Some sessions may be led by individual contractors. Some courses are assigned and organized into sectors which have unique ids. The courses use referenced documents with names. The system can upload/delete documents to/from courses. The school has a unique name.
Functional requirement:
1. Add a course. The school can add a new course by providing a case-sensitive unique ID. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, false.

2. Assign a course to a sector. A course can belong to exactly one sector at a time. When assigning a course to a new sector, the system must first remove it from its current sector (if any) before adding it to the new one. Return true if successful; otherwise, false.

3. Manage the session schedule of a cours. For any course, the system can add or cancel sessions by providing a specific date (format: yyyy-MM-dd). When adding, ensure only one session per course per day.  The operation returns the course's up-to-date list of sessions. When canceling, only sessions without participants may be removed. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, it returns false.

4. Assign or replace a trainer for a session. The trainer assignment or replacement must be done strictly before the session's start date (i.e., the current date must be strictly earlier than the session date; same-day operations are not allowed). The operation returns a boolean value indicating success.

5. Register a participant for a session. A participant may enroll in any session by providing a date and course ID. The call adds the participant to the specified session, ensuring no duplicate enrollments for the same participant. It returns true on success; otherwise, false.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title trainingSchool Model

class Course {
    id : String
    documents : List<Document>
    sector : Sector
    sessions : List<Session>
    addDocument(doc : Document) : boolean
    addSession(date : LocalDate) : List<Session>
    cancelSession(date : LocalDate) : boolean
    removeDocument(doc : Document) : boolean
}
class Document {
    name : String
}
class Participant {
}
abstract class Person {
    id : String
}
class School {
    name : String
    courses : List<Course>
    sectors : List<Sector>
    addCourse(id : String) : boolean
    addSector(id : String) : Sector
    assignCourseToSector(courseId : String, sectorId : String) : boolean
}
class Sector {
    id : String
    courses : List<Course>
    addCourse(course : Course) : void
    removeCourse(course : Course) : void
}
class Session {
    date : LocalDate
    registeredParticipants : List<Participant>
    trainer : Trainer
    assignTrainer(t : Trainer, today : LocalDate) : boolean
    registerParticipant(p : Participant, today : LocalDate) : boolean
    replaceTrainer(newTrainer : Trainer, today : LocalDate) : boolean
}
class Trainer {
    contractor : boolean
}

Course --> "*" Document : documents
Course --> Sector : sector  'opposite: courses
Course *-- "*" Session : sessions
Participant --|> Person
School *-- "*" Course : courses
School *-- "*" Sector : sectors
Sector --> "*" Course : courses  'opposite: sector
Session --> "*" Participant : registeredParticipants
Session --> Trainer : trainer
Trainer --|> Person
@enduml
```

# Requirement
```text
Either in a school that performs the following functions: Develop catalogs that describe the courses and give the estimated dates of the sessions (date format : "2025-02-06"). Each course has an unique id. Register people have their unique id and can participate in the sessions. Determine the trainers who will lead the sessions. Each trainer has an unique id. Some sessions may be led by individual contractors. Some courses are assigned and organized into sectors which have unique ids. The courses use referenced documents with names. The system can upload/delete documents to/from courses. The school has a unique name.
Functional requirement:
1. Add a course. The school can add a new course by providing a case-sensitive unique ID. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, false.

2. Assign a course to a sector. A course can belong to exactly one sector at a time. When assigning a course to a new sector, the system must first remove it from its current sector (if any) before adding it to the new one. Return true if successful; otherwise, false.

3. Manage the session schedule of a cours. For any course, the system can add or cancel sessions by providing a specific date (format: yyyy-MM-dd). When adding, ensure only one session per course per day.  The operation returns the course's up-to-date list of sessions. When canceling, only sessions without participants may be removed. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, it returns false.

4. Assign or replace a trainer for a session. The trainer assignment or replacement must be done strictly before the session's start date (i.e., the current date must be strictly earlier than the session date; same-day operations are not allowed). The operation returns a boolean value indicating success.

5. Register a participant for a session. A participant may enroll in any session by providing a date and course ID. The call adds the participant to the specified session, ensuring no duplicate enrollments for the same participant. It returns true on success; otherwise, false.
```

3. If there is any error, correct the erroneous specification.
4. Follow the specification format. Output changed specifications only; OMIT unchanged specifications.

# Requirement
```text
Either in a school that performs the following functions: Develop catalogs that describe the courses and give the estimated dates of the sessions (date format : "2025-02-06"). Each course has an unique id. Register people have their unique id and can participate in the sessions. Determine the trainers who will lead the sessions. Each trainer has an unique id. Some sessions may be led by individual contractors. Some courses are assigned and organized into sectors which have unique ids. The courses use referenced documents with names. The system can upload/delete documents to/from courses. The school has a unique name.
Functional requirement:
1. Add a course. The school can add a new course by providing a case-sensitive unique ID. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, false.

2. Assign a course to a sector. A course can belong to exactly one sector at a time. When assigning a course to a new sector, the system must first remove it from its current sector (if any) before adding it to the new one. Return true if successful; otherwise, false.

3. Manage the session schedule of a cours. For any course, the system can add or cancel sessions by providing a specific date (format: yyyy-MM-dd). When adding, ensure only one session per course per day.  The operation returns the course's up-to-date list of sessions. When canceling, only sessions without participants may be removed. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, it returns false.

4. Assign or replace a trainer for a session. The trainer assignment or replacement must be done strictly before the session's start date (i.e., the current date must be strictly earlier than the session date; same-day operations are not allowed). The operation returns a boolean value indicating success.

5. Register a participant for a session. A participant may enroll in any session by providing a date and course ID. The call adds the participant to the specified session, ensuring no duplicate enrollments for the same participant. It returns true on success; otherwise, false.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title trainingSchool Model

class Course {
    id : String
    documents : List<Document>
    sector : Sector
    sessions : List<Session>
    addDocument(doc : Document) : boolean
    addSession(date : LocalDate) : List<Session>
    cancelSession(date : LocalDate) : boolean
    removeDocument(doc : Document) : boolean
}
class Document {
    name : String
}
class Participant {
}
abstract class Person {
    id : String
}
class School {
    name : String
    courses : List<Course>
    sectors : List<Sector>
    addCourse(id : String) : boolean
    addSector(id : String) : Sector
    assignCourseToSector(courseId : String, sectorId : String) : boolean
}
class Sector {
    id : String
    courses : List<Course>
    addCourse(course : Course) : void
    removeCourse(course : Course) : void
}
class Session {
    date : LocalDate
    registeredParticipants : List<Participant>
    trainer : Trainer
    assignTrainer(t : Trainer, today : LocalDate) : boolean
    registerParticipant(p : Participant, today : LocalDate) : boolean
    replaceTrainer(newTrainer : Trainer, today : LocalDate) : boolean
}
class Trainer {
    contractor : boolean
}

Course --> "*" Document : documents
Course --> Sector : sector  'opposite: courses
Course *-- "*" Session : sessions
Participant --|> Person
School *-- "*" Course : courses
School *-- "*" Sector : sectors
Sector --> "*" Course : courses  'opposite: sector
Session --> "*" Participant : registeredParticipants
Session --> Trainer : trainer
Trainer --|> Person
@enduml
```

# Operation Specifications
Sector::addCourse
Summary: Adds a course to the sector's course list. Does not update the course's sector reference.
Algorithm:
  1. If course is null or already in courses list, do nothing and return.
  2. Otherwise, add course to courses list.
Related Features: courses (List<Course>)
Related Classes: Course
Input: course : Course – the course to add.
Output: void
Pre-condition: course != null.
Post-condition: The course is added to the sector's courses list if not already present.

Sector::removeCourse
Summary: Removes a course from the sector's course list.
Algorithm:
  1. If course is null or not in courses list, do nothing.
  2. Otherwise, remove course from courses list.
Related Features: courses (List<Course>)
Related Classes: Course
Input: course : Course – the course to remove.
Output: void
Pre-condition: course != null.
Post-condition: The course is removed from the sector's courses list if it was present.

# Operation Specifications
Session::replaceTrainer
Summary: Replaces the current trainer of the session with a new trainer, but only if the current date is strictly before the session's date.
Algorithm:
  1. Validate that newTrainer != null and today != null.
  2. If today.isBefore(this.date) is true, set this.trainer = newTrainer and return true.
  3. Otherwise, return false.
Related Features: trainer (Trainer), date (LocalDate)
Related Classes: Trainer, LocalDate
Input: newTrainer : Trainer – the new trainer; today : LocalDate – the current date.
Output: boolean – true if replacement succeeded; false if today is not strictly before session date.
Pre-condition: newTrainer != null, today != null.
Post-condition: If successful, the session's trainer is set to newTrainer; otherwise, unchanged.

Session::assignTrainer
Summary: Assigns a trainer to the session, but only if the current date is strictly before the session's date.
Algorithm:
  1. Validate that t != null and today != null.
  2. If today.isBefore(this.date) is true, set this.trainer = t and return true.
  3. Otherwise, return false.
Related Features: trainer (Trainer), date (LocalDate)
Related Classes: Trainer, LocalDate
Input: t : Trainer – the trainer to assign; today : LocalDate – the current date.
Output: boolean – true if the trainer was assigned; false if today is not strictly before session date.
Pre-condition: t != null, today != null.
Post-condition: If successful, the session's trainer is set to t; otherwise, unchanged.

Session::registerParticipant
Summary: Registers a participant for the session, ensuring no duplicate enrollment.
Algorithm:
  1. Validate that p != null.
  2. Check if p is already in registeredParticipants list (by ID or reference).
  3. If not duplicate, add p to registeredParticipants list; return true.
  4. Otherwise, return false.
Related Features: registeredParticipants (List<Participant>)
Related Classes: Participant
Input: p : Participant – the participant to register; today : LocalDate – the current date (not used in this specification, but included for future use?).
Output: boolean – true if registered; false if already enrolled.
Pre-condition: p != null.
Post-condition: If not already present, p is added to registeredParticipants; otherwise, unchanged.

5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

1. Read the requirement and the class diagram.
2. For each operation specification, determine whether it is complete and consistent with the requirement.

# Requirement
```text
Either in a school that performs the following functions: Develop catalogs that describe the courses and give the estimated dates of the sessions (date format : "2025-02-06"). Each course has an unique id. Register people have their unique id and can participate in the sessions. Determine the trainers who will lead the sessions. Each trainer has an unique id. Some sessions may be led by individual contractors. Some courses are assigned and organized into sectors which have unique ids. The courses use referenced documents with names. The system can upload/delete documents to/from courses. The school has a unique name.
Functional requirement:
1. Add a course. The school can add a new course by providing a case-sensitive unique ID. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, false.

2. Assign a course to a sector. A course can belong to exactly one sector at a time. When assigning a course to a new sector, the system must first remove it from its current sector (if any) before adding it to the new one. Return true if successful; otherwise, false.

3. Manage the session schedule of a cours. For any course, the system can add or cancel sessions by providing a specific date (format: yyyy-MM-dd). When adding, ensure only one session per course per day.  The operation returns the course's up-to-date list of sessions. When canceling, only sessions without participants may be removed. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, it returns false.

4. Assign or replace a trainer for a session. The trainer assignment or replacement must be done strictly before the session's start date (i.e., the current date must be strictly earlier than the session date; same-day operations are not allowed). The operation returns a boolean value indicating success.

5. Register a participant for a session. A participant may enroll in any session by providing a date and course ID. The call adds the participant to the specified session, ensuring no duplicate enrollments for the same participant. It returns true on success; otherwise, false.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title trainingSchool Model

class Course {
    id : String
    documents : List<Document>

}

    id : String

    addCourse(course : Course) : void
    removeCourse(course : Course) : void
}
class Session {
    date : LocalDate
    registeredParticipants : List<Participant>
    trainer : Trainer
    assignTrainer(t : Trainer, today : LocalDate) : boolean
    registerParticipant(p : Participant, today : LocalDate) : boolean
    replaceTrainer(newTrainer : Trainer, today : LocalDate) : boolean
}
class Trainer {
    contractor : boolean
}

Course --> Sector : sector  'opposite: courses

Sector --> "*" Course : courses  'opposite: sector

Session --> Trainer : trainer
Trainer --|> Person
```

# Operation Specifications
School::addCourse
Summary: Adds a new course to the school's course catalog with a unique case-sensitive ID.
Algorithm:
  1. Validate that id is not null and not empty.
  2. Check if any existing course in courses list has the same id (case-sensitive).
  3. If no duplicate, create a new Course(id) and add it to courses list; return true.
  4. Otherwise, return false.
Related Features: courses (List<Course>)
Related Classes: Course
Input: id : String – the unique case-sensitive identifier for the new course.
Output: boolean – true if the course was added; false if id is invalid or already exists.
Pre-condition: id != null, id.length() > 0.
Post-condition: If successful, a new Course is appended to courses; otherwise, courses unchanged.

School::assignCourseToSector
Summary: Assigns a course to a sector. If the course already belongs to a sector, it is first removed from that sector.
Algorithm:
  1. Find the Course by courseId in the school's courses list. If not found, return false.
  2. Find the Sector by sectorId in the school's sectors list. If not found, return false.
  3. If the course already has a sector (course.sector != null), call sector.removeCourse(course) on its current sector.
  4. Set course.sector = targetSector.
  5. Call targetSector.addCourse(course).
  6. Return true.
Related Features: courses (List<Course>), sectors (List<Sector>), Course::sector, Sector::addCourse, Sector::removeCourse
Related Classes: Course, Sector
Input: courseId : String – the ID of the course; sectorId : String – the ID of the target sector.
Output: boolean – true if assignment succeeded; false if course or sector not found.
Pre-condition: courseId and sectorId are non-null and exist in the school.
Post-condition: The course's sector is updated to the new sector. The old sector no longer contains the course; the new sector does.

School::addSector
Summary: Creates a new sector with a given ID and adds it to the school's sector list.
Algorithm:
  1. Validate that id is not null and not empty.
  2. Check if any existing sector in sectors list has the same id.
  3. If no duplicate, create a new Sector(id) and add it to sectors list; return the new sector.
  4. Otherwise, return null (or throw exception? Here we assume return null on failure).
Related Features: sectors (List<Sector>)
Related Classes: Sector
Input: id : String – the unique identifier for the sector.
Output: Sector – the newly created Sector if successful; null if id is duplicate or invalid.
Pre-condition: id != null, id.length() > 0.
Post-condition: If successful, a new Sector is added to sectors; otherwise, sectors unchanged.

```result
 */

/*
 * revised description for this operation_2
 */
```

# Operation Specifications
Course::addDocument
Summary: Adds a document to the course's document list.
Algorithm:
  1. Check if doc is null or if doc already exists in the documents list (by name).
  2. If not, add doc to documents list.
  3. Return true if added; otherwise, false.
Related Features: documents (List<Document>)
Related Classes: Document
Input: doc : Document – the document to be added; must not be null.
Output: boolean – true if the document was added successfully; false otherwise.
Pre-condition: doc != null, and the course's documents list does not already contain a document with the same name.
Post-condition: If successful, the document is appended to the documents list; otherwise, the list remains unchanged.

Course::addSession
Summary: Adds a new session for the course on the given date. Only one session per course per day is allowed.
Algorithm:
  1. Validate that date is not null.
  2. Check if a session already exists in sessions list with the same date.
  3. If no duplicate, create a new Session(date) and append it to the sessions list.
  4. Return the up-to-date list of sessions (sorted by date).
Related Features: sessions (List<Session>)
Related Classes: Session, LocalDate
Input: date : LocalDate – the date of the session (format: yyyy-MM-dd). Must not be in the past? (unclear, but no restriction given).
Output: List<Session> – the current list of all sessions for the course after the operation.
Pre-condition: date != null; no session with the same date exists in the course's sessions list.
Post-condition: A new Session with the given date is added to sessions. The returned list includes all sessions.

Course::removeDocument
Summary: Removes a document from the course's document list.
Algorithm:
  1. Check if doc is null or not present in the documents list (by reference or name).
  2. If present, remove it; return true.
  3. Otherwise, return false.
Related Features: documents (List<Document>)
Related Classes: Document
Input: doc : Document – the document to be removed.
Output: boolean – true if the document was removed; false otherwise.
Pre-condition: doc != null.
Post-condition: If the document was in the list, it is removed; otherwise, the list remains unchanged.

Course::cancelSession
Summary: Cancels (removes) a session on a given date, but only if the session has no registered participants.
Algorithm:
  1. Find the session in sessions list with the given date.
  2. If found and the session's registeredParticipants list is empty, remove it from sessions list; return true.
  3. Otherwise, return false.
Related Features: sessions (List<Session>), Session::registeredParticipants
Related Classes: Session, LocalDate
Input: date : LocalDate – the date of the session to cancel.
Output: boolean – true if the session was removed; false if not found or has participants.
Pre-condition: date != null.
Post-condition: If the session existed and had no participants, it is removed from sessions; otherwise, sessions unchanged.

Input: doc : Document – the document to be added; must not be null.
Output: boolean – true if the document was added successfully; false otherwise.
Pre-condition: doc != null, and the course's documents list does not already contain a document with the same name.
Post-condition: If successful, the document is appended to the documents list; otherwise, the list remains unchanged.

Course::addSession
Summary: Adds a new session for the course on the given date. Only one session per course per day is allowed.
Algorithm:
  1. Validate that date is not null.
  2. Check if a session already exists in sessions list with the same date.
  3. If no duplicate, create a new Session(date) and append it to the sessions list.
  4. Return the up-to-date list of sessions (sorted by date).
Related Features: sessions (List<Session>)
Related Classes: Session, LocalDate
Input: date : LocalDate – the date of the session (format: yyyy-MM-dd). Must not be in the past? (unclear, but no restriction given).
Output: List<Session> – the current list of all sessions for the course after the operation.
Pre-condition: date != null; no session with the same date exists in the course's sessions list.
Post-condition: A new Session with the given date is added to sessions. The returned list includes all sessions.

Course::removeDocument
Summary: Removes a document from the course's document list.
Algorithm:
  1. Check if doc is null or not present in the documents list (by reference or name).
  2. If present, remove it; return true.
  3. Otherwise, return false.
Related Features: documents (List<Document>)
Related Classes: Document
Input: doc : Document – the document to be removed.
Output: boolean – true if the document was removed; false otherwise.
Pre-condition: doc != null.
Post-condition: If the document was in the list, it is removed; otherwise, the list remains unchanged.

Course::cancelSession
Summary: Cancels (removes) a session on a given date, but only if the session has no registered participants.
Algorithm:
  1. Find the session in sessions list with the given date.
  2. If found and the session's registeredParticipants list is empty, remove it from sessions list; return true.
  3. Otherwise, return false.
Related Features: sessions (List<Session>), Session::registeredParticipants
Related Classes: Session, LocalDate
Input: date : LocalDate – the date of the session to cancel.
Output: boolean – true if the session was removed; false if not found or has participants.
Pre-condition: date != null.
Post-condition: If the session existed and had no participants, it is removed from sessions; otherwise, sessions unchanged.

- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.

    Course foundCourse = null;

      {
        foundCourse = course;
        break;
      }
    }
    if (foundCourse == null)
    {
      return false;

    // Find the sector by sectorId
    Sector foundSector = null;

    {

You will be given a **Java Class** generated from an Ecore model by EMF code generator with `suppressInterfaces=true`. Your task is to implement the Java methods unimplemented by taking additional contextual information into account.

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Compute context information
  1.1 Navigate the Ecore model to find related classes
  1.2 Also consider the related classes in the Javadocs.
  1.3 Locate these files in your project.
  1.4 Generate the class summarization.
2. A method is unimplemented if its body contains `// TODO: Please complete this Java method based on the docstring`.
3. For every unimplemented method, 
  3.1 You must read the docstring of this method. The fragment between `<!-- begin-model-doc -->` and `<!-- end-model-doc -->` contains the method specification;
  3.2 Generate your code strictly according to the algorithm; Be aware of the EMF code style and APIs;
  3.3 You should not change the method signature;
  3.4 After generation, replace the current body with the one you generate;
  3.5 Keep and update the docstring by changing `@generated` to `@generated NOT`.
4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.
5. Do not change other part.
6. Be aware of the method signatures, particularly, the input and output types, provided in the **Context**.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

   *   4. Return the up-to-date list of sessions (sorted by date).
   * Related Features: sessions (List<Session>)
   * Related Classes: Session, LocalDate
   * Input: date : LocalDate – the date of the session (format: yyyy-MM-dd). Must not be in the past? (unclear, but no restriction given).
   * Output: List<Session> – the current list of all sessions for the course after the operation.
   * Pre-condition: date != null; no session with the same date exists in the course's sessions list.
   * Post-condition: A new Session with the given date is added to sessions. The returned list includes all sessions.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model dateDataType="edu.trainingSchool.LocalDate" dateRequired="true"
   *        annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public EList<Session> addSession(final LocalDate date)
  {
    // 1. Validate that date is not null
    if (date == null)
    {
      throw new IllegalArgumentException("date must not be null");
    }
    // 2. Check if a session already exists in sessions list with the same date
    for (Session session : getSessions())
    {
      if (date.equals(session.getDate()))
      {
        // Duplicate found; do not add
        return getSessions();
      }
    }
    // 3. If no duplicate, create a new Session and append it to the sessions list
    Session newSession = TrainingSchoolFactory.eINSTANCE.createSession();
    newSession.setDate(date);
    getSessions().add(newSession);
    // 4. Return the up-to-date list of sessions (sorted by date)
    EList<Session> sortedSessions = getSessions();
    Collections.sort(sortedSessions, (s1, s2) -> s1.getDate().compareTo(s2.getDate()));
    return sortedSessions;
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Cancels (removes) a session on a given date, but only if the session has no registered participants.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. Find the session in sessions list with the given date.
   *   2. If found and the session's registeredParticipants list is empty, remove it from sessions list; return true.
   *   3. Otherwise, return false.

   * Related Classes: Session, LocalDate
   * Input: date : LocalDate – the date of the session to cancel.

   * Post-condition: If the session existed and had no participants, it is removed from sessions; otherwise, sessions unchanged.

   *        annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public boolean cancelSession(final LocalDate date)
  {
    // 1. Find the session in sessions list with the given date
    for (Session session : getSessions())
    {
      if (date.equals(session.getDate()))
      {
        // 2. If found and the session's registeredParticipants list is empty, remove it
        if (session.getRegisteredParticipants().isEmpty())
        {
          getSessions().remove(session);
          return true;
        }
        // 3. Otherwise, return false
        return false;
      }
    }
    // 3. Not found, return false
    return false;
  }

   *        annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"

  {
    // 1. Check if doc is null or if doc already exists in the documents list (by name)

    {
      return false;
    }
    for (Document existingDoc : getDocuments())
    {
      if (doc.getName() != null && doc.getName().equals(existingDoc.getName()))
      {
        return false;
      }
    }
    // 2. If not, add doc to documents list
    getDocuments().add(doc);
    // 3. Return true if added

  }

      return true;
    }
    // 3. Otherwise, return false
    return false;
  }

```java
 * Summary: Adds a course to the sector's course list and updates the course's sector reference.
 * Algorithm:
 *   1. If course is null or already in courses list, do nothing and return.
 *   2. Otherwise, add course to courses list.
 *   3. Set the course's sector to this sector.
 * Related Features: courses (List<Course>)
 * Related Classes: Course
 * Input: course : Course – the course to add.
 * Output: void
 * Pre-condition: course != null.
 * Post-condition: The course is added to the sector's courses list if not already present, and the course's sector attribute is set to this sector.
 * <!-- end-model-doc -->
 * @model courseRequired="true"
```

 * @generated NOT

public void addCourse(final Course course)
{
    if (course == null || getCourses().contains(course))
    {
      return;
    }
    getCourses().add(course);
    course.setSector(this);
}
